### Audit Report: Streaming Tool Output to Discord Implementation

#### Overview
This implementation adds live progress updates for long-running tools (e.g., `exec`, `browser`) in Discord by tracking tool events via `onAgentEvent` callbacks in `agent-runner-execution.ts`, formatting status messages with elapsed time and optional `tail` output, and managing Discord messages via a global `Map` in `tool-status-manager.ts`. Updates are throttled, Discord-formatted, and edited in-place to avoid spam. The flow integrates into Discord's message handler via `onToolStatusUpdate` in `replyOptions`.

Overall, the code is functional and thoughtful (e.g., throttling, tail truncation, Discord limits), but has notable issues in memory safety, races, errors, edges, and maintainability. No critical crashes, but production risks exist.

#### 1. Memory Leaks in Map Tracking
- **Primary Concern: Global `activeStatusMessages` Map (`tool-status-manager.ts`)**:
  - This process-global Map grows unbounded if tools fail/error without reaching "end"/"completed" phase, or if Discord sends/edits fail silently (no fallback cleanup).
  - Entries are deleted only on `isComplete=true` (tool "end") or explicit `clearToolStatusEntry` (unused). No timeout/eviction for stale tools (e.g., crashed gateway restarts mid-tool).
  - Keyed by `channelId:toolCallId`, so per-conversation isolation is good, but in high-volume servers (many channels/tools), Map could bloat to 100s/1000s of entries over time without periodic cleanup.
  - Local Maps in `agent-runner-execution.ts` (`toolStartTimes`, `lastToolStatusUpdate`) are function-scoped and auto-cleaned on "end", so no leak there.
- **Risk Level**: Medium. Leaks slowly; monitor heap via `openclaw logs` or Node inspector.
- **Recommendation**: Add TTL eviction (e.g., `setTimeout` on entry creation to delete after 5min inactivity) or periodic sweep (e.g., cron every 10min deleting entries >2min old without updates). Expose `clearToolStatusEntry` for error paths in `agent-runner`. Consider LRU cache lib like `lru-cache` for bounded size.

#### 2. Race Conditions in Async Operations
- **Map Access During Concurrent Tool Events**:
  - Node.js is single-threaded, but `onAgentEvent` is async and called rapidly from streaming agent events (e.g., multiple "update" phases per tool).
  - Race: "start" event sends new message → sets Map; simultaneous "update" checks `existing` (misses), sends duplicate new message.
    - Fix needed: Atomic check-and-set (but Node lacks mutex; use `compareExchange`-like with timestamp or double-check).
  - Another race: "end" deletes Map concurrently with "update" edit → edit 404s (Discord rejects editing deleted/nonexistent msg).
- **Discord API Concurrency**:
  - Discord rate limits edits/sends (~5/sec per channel). Rapid "update" bursts (e.g., verbose `exec` tail) could hit limits, causing cascading failures without backoff.
  - `sendOrUpdateToolStatus` awaits sequentially per call, but parallel tool events interleave without coordination.
- **Cross-Run Interference**:
  - `dispatchInboundMessage` runs one turn; multiple messages/tools in same channel reuse keys if `toolCallId` collides (unlikely, UUID-based).
- **Risk Level**: High for duplicates/404s in bursty tools (e.g., `exec` with frequent stdout).
- **Recommendation**: Wrap Map ops in a queue/microtask batcher (e.g., `queueMicrotask` debounce per key). Add Discord retry/backoff (exponential, 1-5s). Use `try { await edit } catch { if 404, delete & resend }`.

#### 3. Edge Cases Not Handled
- **Tool Errors/Failures**:
  - `ToolStatusUpdate.status="error"` ignored; no Discord update (stuck "running"). Agent emits "error" phase, but Discord callback skips non-longRunningTools or doesn't handle.
  - No cleanup on agent crash/timeout; orphan messages.
- **Missing/Hidden Data**:
  - `tail` assumes `evt.data.partialResult.details.tail`; if absent/malformed, empty codeblock.
  - `toolCallId` missing/empty → key=`channelId:`, collisions across tools.
  - `elapsedMs=0/undefined` → "running (0s)" awkward.
- **Discord-Specific**:
  - Thread replies: `channelId` from `deliverTarget`, but threads use sub-channel IDs; may post to parent.
  - Ephemeral/DM limits differ; truncation assumes 2000 chars always.
  - Bots can't edit after 15min; old tools stuck.
  - `longRunningTools` hardcoded; misses future tools (e.g., `docker_run`); `update` fires for all.
- **Throttling Gaps**:
  - First update waits 10s rigidly; short tools (<10s) get no update despite "end".
  - No per-channel throttle; noisy channels spam.
- **Config/Env**:
  - `discordConfig.toolStatusUpdates` boolean; no granularity (e.g., per-tool).
  - Tail: 8 lines/1200 chars arbitrary; huge outputs truncate poorly (`slice(-1197)` loses newlines).
- **Risk Level**: Medium. Silent failures (no error msg) confuse users.
- **Recommendation**: Handle "error": append "❌ Failed: [details]". Configurable tools list. Graceful `toolCallId` fallback (e.g., `${toolName}-${Date.now()}`). Test threads/DMs.

#### 4. Error Handling Gaps
- **No Try-Catch in Core Paths**:
  - `sendOrUpdateToolStatus`: Awaits Discord calls; rejects bubble to `onToolStatusUpdate` → unhandled rejection (Node crashes on unhandledRejection if no listener).
  - `agent-runner onAgentEvent`: `void params.opts.onToolStatusUpdate` → swallows errors/logs nothing.
  - `message-handler onToolStatusUpdate`: Awaits but no catch; fails dispatch silently.
- **Discord Failures**:
  - Send/edit 4xx/5xx (rate limit, invalid channel) → no fallback/log.
  - No retry logic.
- **Data Assumptions**:
  - `update.tail` unchecked; could be non-string → crash on `split`.
  - `deliverTarget` parse assumes prefix; fallback `message.channelId` may mismatch threads.
- **Risk Level**: High. Unhandled rejections crash gateway; silent fails leave stale "running".
- **Recommendation**: Wrap all Discord calls: `try { await send/edit } catch(e) { logVerbose(`Tool status ${status} failed: ${e}`); clearToolStatusEntry(...) }`. Add `onError` to `ToolStatusUpdate`. Use `process.on('unhandledRejection')` globally if missing.

#### 5. Code Quality Issues
- **Strengths**: Clean separation (types, manager), good truncation/Discord awareness, throttle prevents spam.
- **Weaknesses**:
  - **Magic Numbers**: 10_000ms delay, 30_000ms throttle, 8 lines, 1200/1990/1197 chars, `\u23F3` – extract to consts (`MIN_UPDATE_MS`, `TAIL_LINES`, `DISCORD_CONTENT_LIMIT=2000`).
  - **Hardcoding**: `longRunningTools` array; make config or regex (e.g., `/^(exec|process|browser|sessions_spawn)/`).
  - **Duplication**: Elapsed formatting in two places (agent-runner summary, Discord content).
  - **Type Safety**: `as Record<string, unknown>` casts unsafe; use Zod/runtime checks for `evt.data`.
  - **Readability**: Nested ifs in `onAgentEvent`; extract `handleToolStatusUpdate(toolEvent)`. Verbose type guards (`typeof evt.data.phase === "string"`).
  - **Globals**: Single Map ok for gateway, but undocumented; add JSDoc on scope/lifecycle.
  - **Tests Missing**: No unit/integration for races, errors, truncation. Add Vitest for `sendOrUpdateToolStatus` mocks.
  - **Perf**: `split("\n").slice(-8).join` O(n) fine, but tail grows unbounded if tool spews.
  - **Consistency**: Summary "Running... (3s)" vs "exec completed"; use template funcs.
- **Risk Level**: Low-Medium. Maintainability suffers; future extensions break.
- **Recommendation**: Refactor to class `ToolStatusTracker` with config ctor. Extract formatters. Add 80% coverage tests (mock Discord, agent events). JSDoc all exports.

#### Summary Recommendations (Prioritized)
1. **Immediate (High Risk)**: Add try-catch + cleanup in `sendOrUpdateToolStatus`/`onToolStatusUpdate`. Fix Map race with debounce.
2. **Short-Term**: Configurable tools/thresholds, error status handling, eviction for Map.
3. **Long-Term**: Per-channel queues, metrics (Prometheus counters for updates/errors/leaks).
4. **No Changes Needed**: Types solid (`ToolStatusUpdate`), integration clean, throttling effective.

Code is 8/10 production-ready post-fixes; solid foundation for other channels (e.g., Slack). Suggest full e2e tests with real Discord bot + long `exec`.

## Code Review: Discord Tool Status Streaming Implementation

### Summary
The implementation adds streaming tool status updates to Discord with a message tracking system. Overall architecture is sound, but there are several areas for improvement regarding memory management, error handling, and edge cases.

### Key Findings

#### 1. Memory Leak Risk in Tool Status Tracking
**Severity: High**

In `agent-runner-execution.ts`, the `toolStartTimes` and `lastToolStatusUpdate` Maps track tool execution but may leak memory:
- Entries are only cleaned up on "end" phase
- If tools crash or don't emit end events, entries persist indefinitely
- No TTL or size limits on Maps

**Recommendation**: Add cleanup mechanisms:
```typescript
// Add periodic cleanup for stale entries
setInterval(() => {
  const now = Date.now();
  const STALE_THRESHOLD = 5 * 60 * 1000; // 5 minutes
  for (const [toolCallId, startTime] of toolStartTimes.entries()) {
    if (now - startTime > STALE_THRESHOLD) {
      toolStartTimes.delete(toolCallId);
      lastToolStatusUpdate.delete(toolCallId);
    }
  }
}, 60 * 1000);
```

#### 2. Global State in Tool Status Manager
**Severity: Medium**

In `tool-status-manager.ts`, `activeStatusMessages` is a global Map that:
- Persists across different message processing cycles
- No cleanup mechanism for completed sessions
- Could accumulate entries over time

**Recommendation**: Consider scoping message tracking to specific sessions or adding TTL-based cleanup.

#### 3. Error Handling Gaps
**Severity: Medium**

In `message-handler.process.ts`, the `onToolStatusUpdate` handler:
- Lacks try/catch around `sendOrUpdateToolStatus`
- Network failures could crash the message processing pipeline
- No retry mechanism for failed status updates

**Recommendation**: Add error boundaries:
```typescript
try {
  await sendOrUpdateToolStatus({
    // ... params
  });
} catch (err) {
  logVerbose(`Discord tool status update failed: ${String(err)}`);
  // Continue processing instead of crashing
}
```

#### 4. Race Conditions in Message Updates
**Severity: Medium**

In `tool-status-manager.ts`:
- No synchronization for concurrent updates to same toolCallId
- Rapid status changes might cause out-of-order message edits
- Discord API rate limits not considered

**Recommendation**: 
- Add queuing mechanism for message updates
- Implement rate limiting per channel
- Consider debouncing frequent updates

#### 5. Edge Case: Missing Channel ID Handling
**Severity: Low**

In `message-handler.process.ts`:
- Fallback to `message.channelId` when deliver target parsing fails
- Could result in status messages sent to wrong channels in complex threading scenarios

#### 6. Code Quality Issues

**Truncation Logic Duplication**:
In `message-handler.process.ts`, the content truncation logic (1990 chars) should be extracted into a reusable function.

**Magic Numbers**:
Several hardcoded values (TOOL_STATUS_THROTTLE_MS, 1200 char limit, 8 lines) should be defined as constants.

**Incomplete Tool Filtering**:
The hardcoded list of "longRunningTools" might miss newly added tools or include inappropriate ones.

### Additional Observations

1. **Tail Output Formatting**: The implementation correctly limits tail output to prevent message size issues
2. **Status Completion**: Proper cleanup of message tracking when tools complete
3. **Throttling**: Appropriate throttling mechanism prevents spamming Discord channels
4. **Selective Tool Updates**: Filtering to only show status for relevant tools is a good optimization

### Recommendations Summary

1. **Critical**: Implement cleanup mechanisms for tracking Maps to prevent memory leaks
2. **High**: Add error handling around Discord API calls
3. **Medium**: Address potential race conditions in message updates
4. **Low**: Extract magic numbers into constants and improve code organization

The implementation provides valuable user feedback during long operations but needs robustness improvements for production use.

## Audit Findings: Streaming Tool Output to Discord

### 1. Memory Leaks in Map Tracking

**Critical Issue: Unbounded Growth in `activeStatusMessages`**
- **Location**: `src/discord/monitor/tool-status-manager.ts` (module-level Map)
- **Problem**: The `activeStatusMessages` Map grows indefinitely if tools error out or if `isComplete` is never set to `true`. The cleanup only happens when `isComplete` is explicitly true, but the status can also be `"error"` (a terminal state).
- **Impact**: Long-running processes will accumulate orphaned entries for every tool that errors or every race condition where completion is missed.

**Secondary Issue: Local Map Accumulation**
- **Location**: `src/auto-reply/reply/agent-runner-execution.ts` (`toolStartTimes`, `lastToolStatusUpdate`)
- **Problem**: While these are function-scoped and will eventually GC, long-running agent turns with many tools could accumulate entries if the "end" phase never fires (e.g., process crash, infinite tool execution).

### 2. Race Conditions in Async Operations

**Critical Race: Duplicate Status Messages**
- **Location**: `src/discord/monitor/tool-status-manager.ts` in `sendOrUpdateToolStatus`
- **Problem**: No synchronization mechanism exists. If two status updates for the same tool arrive simultaneously:
  1. Both calls check `existing` → both get `undefined`
  2. Both send new messages via `sendMessageDiscord`
  3. Second overwrites first in the Map
  4. First message is now orphaned (leaked) and will never be updated/deleted

**Fire-and-Forget Without Error Handling**
- **Location**: `src/auto-reply/reply/agent-runner-execution.ts` line with `void params.opts.onToolStatusUpdate(...)`
- **Problem**: Using `void` to discard the Promise means:
  - Errors are silently swallowed
  - No backpressure handling
  - Updates may arrive out-of-order if the async operation is slower than the event loop

**Async Gap Between Send and Track**
- **Location**: `src/discord/monitor/tool-status-manager.ts`
- **Problem**: The `await sendMessageDiscord()` call could take time. If an update arrives during this window, it will see no `existing` entry and create a duplicate.

### 3. Edge Cases Not Handled

**Discord API Failures**
- **Missing**: Handling for `editMessageDiscord` when the message was deleted by a user/admin during tool execution
- **Missing**: Handling for rate limits (HTTP 429) - no retry logic or exponential backoff
- **Missing**: Validation that `channelId` and `toolCallId` are non-empty strings before using as Map keys

**Tool Lifecycle Edge Cases**
- **Missing**: If a tool errors out (status: `"error"`), the entry in `activeStatusMessages` is never cleaned up because the code only checks `if (params.isComplete)` 
- **Missing**: Tools that complete before the 10-second minimum elapsed time never send any status update (users see nothing)
- **Missing**: If the agent crashes or the process restarts during tool execution, the status message remains stuck in "running" state forever (no TTL)

**Content Truncation Issues**
- **Location**: `src/discord/monitor/message-handler.process.ts`
- **Problem**: Hardcoded truncation to 1990 characters assumes Discord's limit, but doesn't account for:
  - Unicode/grapheme cluster boundaries (could split multi-byte characters)
  - The fact that Discord's limit is 2000 for regular messages, but varies for embeds/webhooks

### 4. Error Handling Gaps

**Silent Failures**
- **Location**: `src/auto-reply/reply/agent-runner-execution.ts` in the "update" phase handler
- **Code**: `void params.opts.onToolStatusUpdate({...})`
- **Issue**: Any error in the Discord sending logic is completely lost. No logging, no metrics, no fallback.

**Unsafe Type Assertions**
- **Location**: `src/auto-reply/reply/agent-runner-execution.ts`
- **Code**: 
  ```typescript
  const partialResult = evt.data.partialResult as Record<string, unknown>;
  const details = partialResult?.details as Record<string, unknown> | undefined;
  const tail = details?.tail as string | undefined;
  ```
- **Issue**: Multiple unsafe casts without validation. If the structure differs, this will throw at runtime.

**Missing Try-Catch in Callback**
- **Location**: `src/discord/monitor/message-handler.process.ts` in `onToolStatusUpdate` callback
- **Issue**: The async callback passed to `dispatchInboundMessage` doesn't wrap `sendOrUpdateToolStatus` in try-catch. If Discord is unreachable, this will throw unhandled.

### 5. Code Quality Issues

**Magic Numbers Everywhere**
- `TOOL_STATUS_THROTTLE_MS = 30_000` (should be configurable)
- `10_000` ms minimum elapsed time (hardcoded)
- `8` lines for tail output (hardcoded)
- `1200` chars output limit (arbitrary)
- `1990` Discord limit (should reference a constant)

**Inconsistent Async Patterns**
- Mix of `await` and `.then().catch()` chains in the same function
- Some errors use `logVerbose`, others use `defaultRuntime.error`, others are silently dropped

**Hardcoded Tool List**
- **Location**: `src/discord/monitor/message-handler.process.ts`
- **Code**: `const longRunningTools = ["exec", "process", "sessions_spawn", "browser"];`
- **Issue**: Violates Open/Closed principle. New tools must manually update this list.

**Global State Without Namespace**
- **Location**: `src/discord/monitor/tool-status-manager.ts`
- **Issue**: The `activeStatusMessages` Map uses simple string concatenation for keys (`${channelId}:${toolCallId}`). If `toolCallId` contains colons or is empty, this could collide or create ambiguous keys.

**Missing Cleanup on Process Exit**
- No `process.on('exit')` or `process.on('SIGINT')` handler to clean up pending status messages or mark them as interrupted.

**No Maximum Size Limit**
- The `activeStatusMessages` Map has no maximum size check. A malicious or buggy scenario with many unique tool calls could exhaust memory.

### Recommendations

1. **Fix Memory Leak**: Clean up Map entries on both `isComplete: true` AND `status: "error"`. Add a TTL mechanism (e.g., `setTimeout` to auto-delete entries older than 1 hour).

2. **Fix Race Condition**: Implement a per-key promise chain or use a concurrency primitive (like `p-limit` with key-based locking) to ensure only one operation per `channelId:toolCallId` runs at a time.

3. **Add Error Handling**: Wrap all Discord API calls in try-catch. If `editMessageDiscord` fails with "Unknown Message", delete the entry from the Map. Log all failures.

4. **Validate Types**: Replace unsafe casts with runtime validation using `zod` or similar, or at least defensive checks (`typeof partialResult?.details === 'object'`).

5. **Make Configurable**: Move magic numbers (throttle, min elapsed time, tail lines) to configuration options.

6. **Add Tests**: Test the race condition scenario (rapid sequential updates) and the error scenario (Discord API throwing).

7. **Consider WeakRef**: If appropriate, consider using `WeakRef` for the message tracking to allow GC if the Discord client drops the reference, though this may not be applicable here.

Here's my audit of the streaming tool output implementation for Discord:

---

### **1. Memory Leaks in Map Tracking**
**Issue Found:**
The `activeStatusMessages` Map in `tool-status-manager.ts` is a **global, module-level** Map that **never gets cleared** except when:
- A tool completes (`isComplete: true`)
- Manual cleanup via `clearToolStatusEntry()`

**Memory Leak Scenarios:**
- **Long-running tools that never complete**: If a tool crashes, times out, or is aborted, its entry remains in the Map indefinitely.
- **Channel/guild cleanup**: If a Discord channel/guild is deleted or the bot leaves, entries for that channel remain in the Map.
- **Bot restart**: The Map is in-memory and resets on restart, but this is not a proper cleanup mechanism.

**Evidence:**
- No cleanup on `abortSignal` or `timeoutMs` in `GetReplyOptions`.
- No cleanup on agent run failure (e.g., `runAgentTurnWithFallback` throws).
- No cleanup on channel/guild deletion events.

**Recommendation:**
- Add cleanup hooks for:
  - Agent run failure (via `try/catch` in `runAgentTurnWithFallback`).
  - Abort signal (`params.opts.abortSignal?.addEventListener("abort", ...)`).
  - Channel/guild deletion (via Discord.js `channelDelete`/`guildDelete` events).
- Add a periodic cleanup task (e.g., every 5 minutes) to remove stale entries (e.g., older than 1 hour).

---

### **2. Race Conditions in Async Operations**
**Issues Found:**
#### **a. Concurrent Tool Status Updates**
- The `onToolStatusUpdate` callback in `message-handler.process.ts` is **not awaited** (called with `void`).
- If multiple updates for the same `toolCallId` arrive in quick succession, they may race to edit the same Discord message.
- Example: A tool emits an update, but before the edit completes, another update arrives and overwrites the first.

**Evidence:**
```ts
void params.opts.onToolStatusUpdate({ ... }); // Not awaited
```

**Recommendation:**
- Use a **per-tool-call-ID queue** to serialize edits.
- Example:
  ```ts
  const toolUpdateQueues = new Map<string, Promise<void>>();

  async function enqueueToolUpdate(update: ToolStatusUpdate) {
    const key = `${update.channelId}:${update.toolCallId}`;
    const existing = toolUpdateQueues.get(key) ?? Promise.resolve();
    const next = existing.then(() => sendOrUpdateToolStatus(update));
    toolUpdateQueues.set(key, next);
    await next;
    if (toolUpdateQueues.get(key) === next) {
      toolUpdateQueues.delete(key);
    }
  }
  ```

#### **b. Map Access in `tool-status-manager.ts`**
- The `activeStatusMessages` Map is accessed/modified **without locks** in `sendOrUpdateToolStatus`.
- If two updates for the same `toolCallId` arrive concurrently, one may overwrite the other.

**Evidence:**
```ts
const existing = activeStatusMessages.get(key); // Race condition here
if (existing) {
  await editMessageDiscord(...); // Second update may overwrite first
}
```

**Recommendation:**
- Use a **per-tool-call-ID lock** (e.g., `AsyncLock` or a `Promise`-based queue) to serialize access to the Map.

---

### **3. Edge Cases Not Handled**
#### **a. Discord API Rate Limits**
- The code does **not handle Discord API rate limits** (e.g., `429 Too Many Requests`).
- If the bot hits a rate limit, `editMessageDiscord`/`sendMessageDiscord` will throw, but the error is **not caught** in `sendOrUpdateToolStatus`.

**Evidence:**
- No `try/catch` around `editMessageDiscord`/`sendMessageDiscord`.
- No retry logic for rate limits.

**Recommendation:**
- Add retry logic with exponential backoff for `429` errors.
- Example:
  ```ts
  async function editWithRetry(channelId: string, messageId: string, content: string, opts: { rest?: RequestClient }) {
    let retries = 3;
    while (retries > 0) {
      try {
        await editMessageDiscord(channelId, messageId, { content }, opts);
        return;
      } catch (err) {
        if (err instanceof Error && err.message.includes("429")) {
          const delay = Math.min(1000 * Math.pow(2, 3 - retries), 10000);
          await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, delay));
          retries--;
        } else {
          throw err;
        }
      }
    }
  }
  ```

#### **b. Message Deletion**
- If a user deletes the status message, the bot **does not detect this** and will fail to edit it.
- The `activeStatusMessages` Map will retain a stale entry.

**Evidence:**
- No handling for `messageDelete` events in Discord.js.

**Recommendation:**
- Listen for `messageDelete` events and clean up the Map:
  ```ts
  client.on("messageDelete", (message) => {
    for (const [key, entry] of activeStatusMessages.entries()) {
      if (entry.messageId === message.id) {
        activeStatusMessages.delete(key);
        break;
      }
    }
  });
  ```

#### **c. Tool Call ID Collisions**
- The `toolCallId` is **not guaranteed to be unique** across different agent runs.
- If two tools with the same `toolCallId` run concurrently, their status updates will collide.

**Evidence:**
- `toolCallId` is generated by the agent (e.g., Pi AI), not the bot.

**Recommendation:**
- Prefix `toolCallId` with the agent `runId` to ensure uniqueness:
  ```ts
  const key = `${params.channelId}:${runId}:${params.toolCallId}`;
  ```

#### **d. Discord Message Length Limits**
- The `content` in `sendOrUpdateToolStatus` is **truncated to 1990 chars**, but:
  - The truncation is **not aware of Markdown** (e.g., may cut off a code block).
  - The `tail` output is **not truncated before joining** (e.g., `update.tail.split("\n").slice(-8)` may exceed limits).

**Evidence:**
```ts
if (content.length > 1990) {
  content = content.slice(0, 1987) + "..."; // May break Markdown
}
```

**Recommendation:**
- Use a **Markdown-aware truncation** function (e.g., ensure code blocks are closed).
- Truncate the `tail` **before** joining:
  ```ts
  const lines = update.tail.split("\n").slice(-8);
  const output = lines.join("\n").slice(-1200); // Truncate before joining
  ```

---

### **4. Error Handling Gaps**
#### **a. Silent Failures in `onToolStatusUpdate`**
- Errors in `onToolStatusUpdate` are **swallowed** (called with `void`).
- Example: If `sendOrUpdateToolStatus` throws, the error is **not logged**.

**Evidence:**
```ts
void params.opts.onToolStatusUpdate({ ... }); // Errors are lost
```

**Recommendation:**
- Add error logging:
  ```ts
  try {
    await params.opts.onToolStatusUpdate({ ... });
  } catch (err) {
    logVerbose(`Tool status update failed: ${String(err)}`);
  }
  ```

#### **b. No Error Handling in `sendOrUpdateToolStatus`**
- If `editMessageDiscord` or `sendMessageDiscord` throws, the error is **not caught**.
- The `activeStatusMessages` Map may become **inconsistent** (e.g., message fails to send, but entry is added to the Map).

**Evidence:**
```ts
await editMessageDiscord(...); // No try/catch
activeStatusMessages.set(key, { ... }); // May run even if edit fails
```

**Recommendation:**
- Wrap Discord API calls in `try/catch` and **revert** the Map on failure:
  ```ts
  try {
    await editMessageDiscord(...);
  } catch (err) {
    if (existing) {
      activeStatusMessages.delete(key); // Revert on failure
    }
    throw err;
  }
  ```

#### **c. No Timeout for Tool Status Updates**
- There is **no timeout** for `onToolStatusUpdate` or `sendOrUpdateToolStatus`.
- If the Discord API hangs, the bot may **block indefinitely**.

**Evidence:**
- No `AbortSignal` or timeout in `sendOrUpdateToolStatus`.

**Recommendation:**
- Add a timeout (e.g., 10 seconds) for Discord API calls:
  ```ts
  const controller = new AbortController();
  const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 10000);
  try {
    await editMessageDiscord(..., { signal: controller.signal });
  } finally {
    clearTimeout(timeout);
  }
  ```

---

### **5. Code Quality Issues**
#### **a. Magic Numbers**
- **Hardcoded values** like `1990`, `1200`, `10_000`, `30_000` are **not documented**.
- Example:
  ```ts
  const TOOL_STATUS_THROTTLE_MS = 30_000; // No explanation
  ```

**Recommendation:**
- Replace with **named constants** and document:
  ```ts
  const DISCORD_MESSAGE_MAX_LENGTH = 2000;
  const TOOL_STATUS_THROTTLE_MS = 30_000; // Max 1 update per 30s per tool
  ```

#### **b. Type Safety Issues**
- **No runtime validation** for `ToolStatusUpdate` or `SendOrUpdateToolStatusParams`.
- Example: `toolCallId` is assumed to be a `string`, but could be `undefined`.

**Evidence:**
```ts
const toolCallId = typeof evt.data.toolCallId === "string" ? evt.data.toolCallId : "";
```

**Recommendation:**
- Use **Zod** or **TypeScript type guards** to validate inputs:
  ```ts
  const ToolStatusUpdateSchema = z.object({
    toolName: z.string(),
    toolCallId: z.string(),
    status: z.enum(["running", "completed", "error"]),
    // ...
  });
  ```

#### **c. Duplicate Logic**
- The **tool status formatting** logic is duplicated in:
  - `agent-runner-execution.ts` (for `onToolStatusUpdate`).
  - `message-handler.process.ts` (for `onToolStatusUpdate` callback).

**Recommendation:**
- Extract into a **shared utility function**:
  ```ts
  export function formatToolStatusUpdate(update: ToolStatusUpdate): string {
    let content = `\u23F3 **${update.toolName}** ${update.status === "completed" ? "completed" : `running (${Math.round((update.elapsedMs ?? 0) / 1000)}s)`}`;
    if (update.tail && update.status !== "completed") {
      const lines = update.tail.split("\n").slice(-8);
      let output = lines.join("\n").slice(-1200);
      content += `\n\`\`\`\n${output}\n\`\`\``;
    }
    return content.slice(0, 1990);
  }
  ```

#### **d. No Cleanup on Agent Run Failure**
- If `runAgentTurnWithFallback` throws, **no cleanup** is performed for:
  - `toolStartTimes`/`lastToolStatusUpdate` Maps.
  - `activeStatusMessages` entries.

**Evidence:**
- No `finally` block in `runAgentTurnWithFallback`.

**Recommendation:**
- Add a `finally` block to clean up:
  ```ts
  try {
    // ... agent run logic
  } finally {
    toolStartTimes.clear();
    lastToolStatusUpdate.clear();
    // Clean up activeStatusMessages for this run
    for (const toolCallId of toolStartTimes.keys()) {
      clearToolStatusEntry(channelId, toolCallId);
    }
  }
  ```

#### **e. No Logging for Tool Status Updates**
- There is **no logging** for tool status updates (e.g., when a tool starts/completes).
- Debugging tool issues is **difficult** without logs.

**Evidence:**
- No `logVerbose` calls in `onToolStatusUpdate`.

**Recommendation:**
- Add debug logging:
  ```ts
  logVerbose(`Tool ${update.toolName} (${update.toolCallId}) status: ${update.status}`);
  ```

---

### **Summary of Critical Issues**
| Category               | Issue                                                                 | Severity |
|------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------|----------|
| Memory Leaks           | Global `activeStatusMessages` Map never cleaned up                   | High     |
| Race Conditions        | Concurrent edits to the same Discord message                         | High     |
| Edge Cases             | No handling for message deletion, rate limits, or tool ID collisions | High     |
| Error Handling         | Silent failures in `onToolStatusUpdate` and Discord API calls        | High     |
| Code Quality           | Magic numbers, duplicate logic, no cleanup on failure                | Medium   |

---

### **Recommended Fixes**
1. **Memory Leaks**:
   - Add cleanup hooks for abort signals, agent failures, and channel/guild deletions.
   - Add a periodic cleanup task for stale entries.

2. **Race Conditions**:
   - Use a per-tool-call-ID queue to serialize edits.
   - Add locks for Map access.

3. **Edge Cases**:
   - Handle Discord API rate limits with retries.
   - Listen for `messageDelete` events to clean up the Map.
   - Prefix `toolCallId` with `runId` to avoid collisions.
   - Use Markdown-aware truncation for Discord messages.

4. **Error Handling**:
   - Add `try/catch` around `onToolStatusUpdate` and Discord API calls.
   - Add timeouts for Discord API calls.

5. **Code Quality**:
   - Replace magic numbers with named constants.
   - Add runtime validation for inputs.
   - Extract duplicate logic into shared functions.
   - Add cleanup in `finally` blocks.
